Document 001: -335亿美元！亚马逊的这个数字，“吓蒙”了市场

Source PDF: all report pdf/-335亿美元！亚马逊的这个数字，“吓蒙”了市场.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-10-30
Pages: 5
Status: ok

### Page 1

亚⻢逊三季度⾃由现⾦流骤降⾄ -335 亿美元，较市场预期 -77 亿美元超出四倍，背后是单季
773 亿资本开⽀与 481 亿新增债务的双重冲击。 AWS 增速虽创五年新⾼达 39.3% ，却仍低于
买⽅预期，利润率反向下滑。四季度运营利润指引 310 亿，全⾯低于华尔街及买⽅预期。核
⼼症结在于： AWS 每赚 1 美元，背后捆绑近 4 美元新增⽀出。
摘要：
亚⻢逊三季度财报⽤⼀个极其惨烈的数字回答了华尔街全年的疑问：单季⾃由现⾦流消耗⾼达
335 亿美元。尽管 AWS 云业务创下近五年最快增速，但失控的资本⽀出（ Capex ）、疲软的四季
度指引以及暴增的债务，彻底击碎了买⽅的乐观预期。
亚⻢逊最新财报显⽰，三季度⾃由现⾦流录得 -335 亿美元，⽽彭博市场⼀致预期仅为 -77 亿美
元，实际结果超出预期四倍以上。 单季资本开⽀飙升⾄ 773 亿美元，环⽐增⻓ 43% ；为填补资
⾦缺⼝，公司在该季度新增 481 亿美元⻓期债务。滚动 12 个⽉⾃由现⾦流从上季度的 -76 亿美元

急剧恶化⾄ -424 亿美元。
与此同时，四季度业绩指引令市场失望。营收指引中值为 2435 亿美元，略低于市场⼀致预期的
2442 亿美元；运营利润指引中值 310 亿美元，不仅低于华尔街预期的 330 亿美元，更全⾯低于买
⽅层⾯ 350 亿美元的 " ⼼理关⼝ " 。 ⾼盛在盘后快评中指出，多项核⼼指标相对于市场预期呈
现 " 中性偏弱 " 结果，预计股价将出现初步负⾯反应。
财报公布后，亚⻢逊盘后股价⼀度暴跌 12.5% ⾄ 223 美元附近（最低触及约 214 美元），远超
7.5% 的隐含波动率，抹平了⼆季度以来的全部涨幅，回落⾄今年 4 ⽉初⽔平。
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⻅闻历 搜索

### Page 2

对于投资者⽽⾔，核⼼逻辑已经发⽣根本性转变：亚⻢逊的股票之所以 “ 涨不动 ” ，是因为 AWS
业务每带来 1 美元的潜在收益，背后都捆绑着远超 1 美元（甚⾄接近 4 美元）的新增⽀出。华尔街
的预期游戏已经失效，现在市场必须直⾯⾼昂的账单。
那个关键数字：单季 -335 亿美元
如果说有⼀个数字概括了亚⻢逊当前的处境，那就是-335 亿美元—— 这是公司三季度单季⾃由
现⾦流。
这⼀数字的背后逻辑极为直⽩：单季经营性现⾦流约 424 亿美元，但净资本开⽀⾼达 759 亿美
元，两者之差即为现⾦⿊洞。⽽就在⼀年前，亚⻢逊的⾃由现⾦流还处于 529 亿美元的历史峰
值。
资本开⽀的膨胀速度同样触⽬惊⼼。三季度⽑资本开⽀达 773 亿美元，环⽐⼆季度的 542 亿美元
增⻓ 43% 。亚⻢逊同时确认，预计 2026 年全年资本开⽀约 2200 亿美元的计划不变。按前三季度
已累计净⽀出约 1720 亿美元计算，四季度资本开⽀将约为 480 亿美元，较刚刚完成的三季度骤降
约 37% 。
这⼀测算令投资者⽣疑：要么亚⻢逊将在四季度⼤幅收紧⽀出，与 CEO Andy Jassy 所描述的 " 需

求持续超过供给 " 形成⽭盾；要么三季度存在⼤量⽀出的提前确认，使得原有指引本⾝已失效。
⽆论哪种情形，市场均难以认同 2027 年资本开⽀将低于 2026 年 —— 此前买⽅预期明年资本开⽀
在 3200 亿⾄ 3750 亿美元之间，⽽亚⻢逊在本次财报中对 2027 年的数字只字未提。
谁来买单？债务飙升，资产负债表急剧恶化

### Page 3

当运营现⾦⽆法覆盖⽀出时，钱只能从别处来。亚⻢逊的资产负债表给出了答案：疯狂发债。
为了⽀撑庞⼤的开⽀，亚⻢逊在三季度筹集了 481 亿美元的新增⻓期债务。今年前九个⽉，公司
新增⻓期借款⾼达 1151 亿美元，导致⻓期债务⼏乎翻了三倍，从 2025 年底的 656 亿美元飙升⾄ 9
⽉底的 1731 亿美元。
与此同时，公司的财务健康度正在全⾯下滑：
现⾦储备从 868 亿美元骤降⾄ 558 亿美元。
利息⽀出同⽐翻了三倍多，从去年同期的 5 亿美元飙升⾄ 17 亿美元。
“ 其他⻓期负债 ” 也⼏乎翻了三倍，从 360 亿美元增⾄ 999 亿美元。
值得注意的是，上述数据还不包括亚⻢逊⾸次发⾏的英镑计价债券，以及⽤于购买英伟达芯⽚
的 80 亿美元特殊⽬的载体安排。
正如市场所观察到的逻辑链条： “债务变为资本开⽀，资本开⽀变为营收，营收变为每股收益。 ”
亚⻢逊本季度在规模上完整展⽰了这条链条的第⼀个环节。
AWS 与核⼼业务：五年最佳增速仍难填买⽅胃⼝
尽管 AWS 云业务表现强劲，营收增⻓ 39.3% ⾄ 460 亿美元，创下近五年最快增速，且 CEO Jassy

给出了 2027 和 2028 年 AWS 增⻓ “ 超过 40%” 的乐观预期。但这对挑剔的买⽅来说还不够：39.3%
的增速虽然击败了华尔街约 37% 的预期，但低于买⽅真正期待的 42% 。
更糟糕的是利润率⾛向了错误的⽅向。AWS 营业利润率从⼆季度的 39.4% 下滑⾄ 38.7%，未能
达到买⽅预期的 “40% 出头 ” 。
其他业务⽅⾯，零售表现平稳（北美销售额增⻓ 6% ，国际增⻓ 8% ，剔除汇率影响）。但⼴告业
务增速放缓⾄ 12%（剔除汇率影响），公司将其归咎于 Prime Day 的 “ 时间异常 ” ，并承诺四季度将
反弹⾄⾼⼗位数增⻓。
此外，每股收益（ EPS ）录得 1.89 美元，不及预期的 1.97 美元。这主要是因为去年三季度包含了
Anthropic 股份带来的 95 亿美元税前收益，⽽今年⾮运营项⽬反⽽造成了 12 亿美元的拖累（包含

中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。

翻倍的利息账单）。唯⼀符合预期的是营业利润，达到创纪录的 281 亿美元（利润率 13.9% ），精
准命中买⽅传⾔的 280 亿美元。
疲软的四季度指引：全⾯不及预期
四季度业绩指引是此次财报的另⼀个失望来源。
营收指引区间为 2400 亿⾄ 2470 亿美元，中值 2435 亿美元，略低于市场⼀致预期的 2442 亿美
元。运营利润指引为 285 亿⾄ 335 亿美元，中值 310 亿美元，不仅低于华尔街预期的 330 亿美元，
也低于买⽅⼼理关⼝的 300 亿⾄ 350 亿美元区间的两端，缺⼝超过 15 亿美元。
据摩根⼤通分析师 Mark Schilsky 此前预测，⼀旦亚⻢逊内部 AI 助⼿ Leo 正式上线、相关成本开始
资本化，运营利润将获得约 10 亿美元的额外提振 —— 即便如此，实际指引仍未能达到预期。

⼴告业务是指引中唯⼀的亮点：三季度⼴告增速放缓⾄ 12% （剔除汇率影响），公司将此归因于
Prime Day 的 “ 时间性异常 ” ，并预计四季度将反弹⾄ ” ⾼⼗字头 ” 增速区间。不过，随着亚⻢逊屏
蔽 Meta Muse 等 AI 购物代理、市场担忧 AI 代理购物将侵蚀赞助商品⼴告收⼊，⼴告业务的⻓期前
景仍存争议。
为什么这只股票 “ 永远涨不动 ” ？
摩根⼤通分析师 Mark Schilsky 在财报前已对亚⻢逊的困境作出精准诊断：
据⾼盛和瑞银的评估，亚⻢逊财报前是科技股中拥挤度最⾼的多头头⼨之⼀，⾼盛 TMT 桌拥挤
度评分 8/10 ，瑞银情绪量表 9/10 。⾼度拥挤的多头持仓意味着，⼀旦现实未能匹配预期，⽌损卖
压将成倍放⼤。
三季度财报的结构性⽭盾清晰可⻅： AWS 加速兑现，但每⼀美元的云业务收⼊，背后跟随着远
超⼀美元的新增⽀出。本季度，这⼀⽐例接近四⽐⼀。  只要这⼀模式未能扭转，股价上涨的逻
辑便始终⾯临来⾃资产负债表的压制。
这张账单是：单季 773 亿美元的资本⽀出、 335 亿美元的现⾦消耗，以及 481 亿美元的新增债

务。亚⻢逊的股票之所以表现挣扎，是因为 AWS 带来的每⼀美元上⾏空间，都捆绑着超过⼀美
元的新增⽀出 —— ⽽最新财报显⽰，这个⽐例接近⼀⽐四。
市场终于意识到，⾼昂的基础设施投⼊正在⽆情地吞噬股东的现⾦流。
“AMZN 是今年最让主流投资者沮丧的股票之⼀ …… 华尔街对 2027 年 AWS 的预测低得可笑 …… 正
因如此，投资者都把它视为容易超预期的故事，但所有⼈都知道这⼀点，这恰恰是它涨不动的核
⼼原因。 ”

通膨升溫壓抑美股表現，近期聚焦財報結果
2026 年 10 月 15 日
一、 市場動態
1. 科技股領跌，費半連 6 日收黑：美股主要指數 10 月 14 日全數下跌，道瓊工業指數下
跌 0.09%，收 51,621.48 點；S&P 500 指數下跌 1.11%，收 7,718.94 點，為 7 月 29
日以來最大單日跌幅；Nasdaq 綜合指數下跌 1.88%，收 26,692.87 點，為 7 月 23
日以來最大單日跌幅。費城半導體指數下跌 4.30%，收 11,684.38 點，連續第６個交
易日走低，為 2025 年 5 月以來最長連跌紀錄。

2. 類股非全面下挫，資金仍呈輪動現象：半導體及 AI 相關股票在 10/14 跌幅尤其明顯
，AMD 下跌 5.73%、Nvidia 下跌 3.16%、Broadcom 下跌 3.10%，AI 基礎建設相關
的 CoreWeave、Nebius、Oracle 及 Dell 跌幅更深，顯示市場對資本支出規模較大、
融資成本敏感度較高的 AI 相關企業風險偏好明顯下降。不過，S&P 500 的 11 大類
股中有 8 類下跌，資訊科技及通訊服務為主要拖累來源，但醫療保健、必需消費與能
源類股仍逆勢上漲。
3. 美國 10 年期公債殖利率盤中急升後回落：美國 10 年期公債殖利率 10/14 由 5.2%左
右快速升至約 5.33%，逼近近期高點；然而，隨後市場消化 Fed 官員較審慎的政策訊
號，殖利率自高檔回落，終場約收於 5.19%，較前一交易日 5.21%小幅下降約 2 個基
點。
二、 下跌原因分析
1. 美 9 月通膨高於預期 ，Fed 再升息風險重新升高 ：美國 10/14 公布 9 月 CPI 月增 0.6%

、年增率也由 8 月的 3.4%上揚至 3.7%；核心 CPI 月增 0.3%、年增率由 8 月的 2.4%
升至 2.6%。其中汽油價格單月大漲 9.1%，貢獻整體 CPI 月增幅超過一半；住宅成本
亦月增 0.4%，顯示物價壓力仍具黏性。此數據公布後，市場重新提高 Fed 進一步升
息的預期 ，CME FedWatch 所反映的 10 月升息 1 碼機率一度由前一日約 22%提高至
37%。雖然截至 10/15 台北時間早上 7 點左右該機率回降至 29.3%，這也提醒投資人
Fed 的升息路徑仍存在不確定性。
2. 高利率同時壓縮科技股估值與提高 AI 融資成本 ：科技及半導體公司未來獲利成長占評
價的比重較高，因此對折現率變化相對敏感。當長天期美債殖利率維持 5%以上，市
場願意支付的本益比自然受到限制，即使企業獲利仍維持成長，股價也可能因評價倍
數下降而出現修正。另一方面，本輪 AI 投資需要大量資料中心、伺服器、電力與網路
基礎設施，高利率亦直接增加企業融資成本，反映信用市場對高度依賴外部資金的 AI

基礎建設公司要求更高風險溢酬。

### Page 2

確性不做任何擔保，如有錯漏或疏忽，本行及本行之任何董事或受僱人，毋須負任何法律責任。任何人因信賴此等資料而做出或改變投資決
策，應審慎衡量本身之需求及投資風險，自行承擔一切投資風險並自負盈虧，本行不負任何法律責任。本報告受中華民國著作權法保護為元
大銀行所有，非經本行同意，不得將本報告內容之一部或全部加以複製、轉載或散布。本報告所載述的意見本行可隨時予以更改或撤回，恕
不另行通知。
3. AI 投資焦點由「支出規模」轉向「投資報酬率」:近期市場對 AI 的疑慮逐漸由「需求
是否成長」 ，轉向「龐大的資本支出能否轉化為足夠營收、獲利與自由現金流」 。
Broadcom 執行長陳福陽近期亦指出 ，AI 未來發展的重要限制可能逐漸由晶片或記憶
體供應轉向資本本身。我們認為，這並不代表AI 資本支出週期即將結束，而是市場已
由第一階段的 「資本支出愈大、成長想像愈高」 ，逐步進入更重視資本效率與投資報酬
率的第二階段。未來財務結構、現金流與AI 商業化能力，將成為不同企業評價分化的
重要因素。
4. 中國記憶體競爭疑慮加重半導體短線壓力：中國長鑫存儲（CXMT）10/10 傳出記憶

體技術取得進展 ， 引發市場對未來DRAM 供給增加及價格競爭的疑慮 ， 記憶體相關類
股率先承壓，並進一步影響整體半導體市場情緒。惟 DRAM 與 GPU、客製化 ASIC、
先進晶圓製程及先進封裝的產業競爭與需求結構並不相同。我們認為，儘管記憶體競
爭升溫值得關注，但目前尚不足以延伸為整體 AI 半導體需求同步轉弱。
5. 高檔籌碼加速獲利了結：AI 與半導體仍是 2026 年美股表現最強勢的投資主題之一，
部分科技股今年累計漲幅已相當可觀。當通膨、殖利率及AI 投資報酬疑慮同時升高，
先前漲幅較大、投資人部位較集中的族群自然成為優先調節對象。因此，本波費半快
速修正除了總經與基本面疑慮外，亦包含籌碼降溫及風險部位收縮因素，使短期價格
波動幅度可能高於企業基本面實際變化。
三、市場看法
1. 類股輪動明顯，尚非全面看空美股：科技與 AI 相關股票 10 月 14 日明顯承壓，但醫
療保健、必需消費與能源類股仍逆勢上漲，道瓊工業指數僅下跌 0.09%。此外，S&P
500 成分股下跌家數與上漲家數之比約為 1.1：1；相較於指數下跌 1.11%，市場廣度

並未同步大幅惡化。這反映資金主要由高評價且前期漲幅較大的科技與AI 族群，轉向
現金流較穩定的防禦型產業。現階段市場特徵較接近類股輪動與風格切換，而非無差
別的全面風險撤離，亦尚未看到美股企業獲利或經濟基本面全面反轉的明確訊號。
2. 美股科技股財報前風險降載，Q3 仍存在重新定價空間：近期科技股修正，費城半導
體指數連續６個交易日下跌，也可能部分反映第三季財報季正式展開前，投資人在股
價先前漲幅較大、評價偏高及利率環境不確定下，提前降低風險曝險。真正決定後續
市場方向的關鍵 ， 仍在於實際企業獲利能否支持目前的高成長預期 。根據 Bloomberg
季度獲利成長追蹤資料，今年 Q1 與 Q2 財報季期間的 EPS 成長率經驗顯示，隨實際
財報陸續公布，企業獲利成長曾明顯優於財報季開始前的市場預期。儘管這並不保證
Q3 將複製前兩季的上修幅度，但目前市場因財報不確定性所反映的風險折價仍存在
被實際財報重新驗證的可能。若大型科技及半導體公司後續營收、獲利與AI 需求指引
實際公布結果優於預期，近期風險溢價可望部分收斂；反之，若獲利成長無法跟上目

前評價，市場整理時間則可能進一步延長。

確性不做任何擔保，如有錯漏或疏忽，本行及本行之任何董事或受僱人，毋須負任何法律責任。任何人因信賴此等資料而做出或改變投資決

大銀行所有，非經本行同意，不得將本報告內容之一部或全部加以複製、轉載或散布。本報告所載述的意見本行可隨時予以更改或撤回，恕
不另行通知。
3. 費半修正幅度大，但 AI 需求尚未出現全面反轉：ASML 在 10/14 所公布的最新第三
季財報反而顯示 AI 相關設備需求仍相當強勁，該公司表示 2027 與 2028 年規劃產能
可能仍無法完全滿足客戶需求，部分客戶亦已表達對 2029 及 2030 年設備需求的興
趣。儘管費城半導體指數近期市場情緒轉弱，但由 ASML 的財報表現來看，目前仍未
看到大型 AI 客戶全面取消晶片訂單、主要雲端服務商同步削減 AI 資本支出，或先進
製程需求快速降溫等結構性訊號。
4. 台積電營收顯示 AI 需求暢旺 ， 留意10/15 法說會內容：台積電 10/8 公布的第三季營

收達新台幣 1.49 兆元、年增約 50%，創歷史新高，並高於市場原先預估的 1.46 兆元
；9 月單月營收年增 54.6%。強勁的營收表現仍反映 AI 與先進製程需求具有支撐。目
前市場因此呈現「企業營收仍快速成長，但股票評價下降」的狀況。高殖利率環境下
，EPS 上升與本益比下降可以同時發生，後續股價方向將取決於企業獲利上修幅度是
否足以抵銷評價收縮。該公司將於 10/15 下午舉行第三季法人說明會。市場重點將集
中於第四季展望、AI 與 HPC 需求、先進製程、先進封裝及未來資本支出規劃。公司
對 AI 需求與產能投資的最新判斷，將成為判斷近期費半修正主要屬於評價調整，或產
業基本面是否開始出現變化的重要依據。
5. AI 與半導體進入基本面分化階段：我們認為，在高利率環境下，高度仰賴外部融資、
自由現金流尚未成熟的 AI 基礎建設業者 ， 受到的資金成本壓力明顯高於具穩定現金流
的大型科技與雲端服務商。同樣地，記憶體、GPU、ASIC、先進製程及先進封裝各有
不同供需結構。我們預期下一階段 AI 行情將更加重視訂單能見度、產品競爭力、毛利

率、自由現金流及 AI 營收實際兌現，產業內部強弱分化程度可能進一步提高。
6. Fed 路徑仍存不確定性，惟企業獲利可望提供股市支撐：美國 9 月 CPI 升溫使市場重
新評估 Fed 後續升息時點與幅度，美國利率政策仍存在不確定性，長天期美債殖利率
也可能維持高檔。不過，現階段企業獲利成長與AI 相關需求仍具韌性，若第三季財報

### Page 4

確性不做任何擔保，如有錯漏或疏忽，本行及本行之任何董事或受僱人，毋須負任何法律責任。任何人因信賴此等資料而做出或改變投資決
策，應審慎衡量本身之需求及投資風險，自行承擔一切投資風險並自負盈虧，本行不負任何法律責任。本報告受中華民國著作權法保護為元
大銀行所有，非經本行同意，不得將本報告內容之一部或全部加以複製、轉載或散布。本報告所載述的意見本行可隨時予以更改或撤回，恕
不另行通知。
延續前兩季優於預期的趨勢，企業 EPS 若能上修有望部分抵銷高利率對本益比的壓縮
效果。換言之，後續市場表現未必只由 Fed 政策主導，企業實際獲利能否持續兌現，
將是支撐科技與半導體股中期走勢的重要力量。
四、投資建議
1. 短線聚焦美股財報與 Fed 決策，科技股中期趨勢料不致有變：近期市場同時面臨財報
前的觀望態勢、美債殖利率偏高及 AI 資本支出疑慮等不確定因素，短線風險偏好可能
受到壓抑，後續宜關注 10 月美股財報對企業獲利與資本支出展望，以及 FOMC 會議
所釋出的政策訊號。中期而言，目前AI 相關企業營運與資本支出尚未出現全面轉弱跡

象，科技產業基本面仍具一定支撐，但在利率維持高檔及市場評價偏高的環境下，後
續宜更加重視企業獲利、現金流及資本支出的實際變化。
2. 中天期債券風險報酬仍較均衡 ：美國 9 月 CPI 升溫使短端殖利率持續受到 Fed 政策路
徑影響，而 20～30 年超長端仍面臨財政赤字、國債供給與期限溢價偏高等結構性壓
力。相較之下，目前殖利率已位於歷史相對高檔，若後續核心通膨或能源價格改善，
中段殖利率具有回落條件，同時其存續期間風險又低於超長端，因此現階段以中天期
美國公債及投資級債的風險報酬結構相對較佳。

leverage. We lift our earnings projection for 2026/2027 /28 by 9%/ 25%/9%. We
maintain our Buy with TP$6,800 (30x of 2027 /28 EPS, from 35x previously, mainly

### Page 7

Mediatek (2454.TW)
21 October 2026 Citi Research
7
Mediatek
Company description
Mediatek (MTK) is a fabless IC company that designs SoC for smartphone,
smart TV, and digital media devices. It also develops chips for connectivity,
networking, smart home gadgets, and automotive sectors. It was founded in
1997, is listed on the Taiwan Stock Exchange, and is headquartered in Taiwan.
It has sales and research subsidiaries in mainland China, Singapore, India,

the US, Japan, Korea, Denmark, and England. It primarily sells its products in
China and other emerging-market countries.
Investment strategy
We rate Mediatek shares as Buy. We expect Mediatek to ride on 5G migration
in emerging markets and market share gain of its high-end Dimensity series in
China market as well. In particular, we see strong growth potential from its AI
ASIC, NTN-satellite business in the long term. We believe solid fundamentals

and strong operating leverage will support Mediatek’s margins outlook.
Valuation
Our NT$6,800 target price (rounded) for Mediatek is based on 30x P/E to
2027 /2028 EPS, which is 1-std above its five-year average forward P/E given
the promising outlook from its AI ASIC business starting from 2H26. We
believe our target PE is also justified by Mediatek's global and regional peers'
trading at an average of c32x PER. AI ASIC development with CSPs should

provide more upside from 2027 onwards. We also see Mediatek as well
prepared for 6G and 6G+NTN Satellite communication. Despite lukewarm
smartphone overall deamand, we expect Mediatek to benefit from 5G
migration in emerging markets and its Dimensity series should also gain
shares in flagship models. Non-smartphone business is also seeing
stabilizing TV and Io T demand with Mediatek outgrowing the overall market
on its better technology in WiFi 7 and connectivity.

Risks
Key downside risks that could prevent the shares from reaching our target
price include: 1) weaker-than-expected growth from AI ASIC business; 2)
more severe competition in the smartphone segment; 3) slower overall
demand related to macroeconomic risks, especially for smartphone demand;
and 4) further price hikes from foundries.
Prepared for Kevin Lu

compensation that is impacted by overall Firm profitability which includes investment banking, sales and trading, and
principal trading revenues. One factor in equity research analyst compensation is arranging corporate access events between

institutional clients and the management teams of covered companies. Typically, company management is more likely to
participate when the analyst has a positive view of the company.

Guide to Citi Research Fundamental Research Investment Ratings:
Citi Research stock recommendations include an investment rating and an optional risk rating to highlight high risk stocks.
Risk rating takes into account both price volatility and fundamental criteria. Stocks will either have no risk rating or a High risk
rating assigned.
Investment Ratings: Citi Research investment ratings are Buy, Neutral and Sell. Our ratings are a function of analyst

expectations of expected total return ("ETR") and risk. ETR is the sum of the forecast price appreciation (or depreciation) plus
the dividend yield for a stock within the next 12 months.  The target price is based on a 12 month time horizon. The Investment
rating definitions are: Buy (1) ETR of 15% or more or 25% or more for High risk stocks; and Sell (3) for negative ETR. Any covered
stock not assigned a Buy or a Sell is a Neutral (2). For stocks rated Neutral (2), if an analyst believes that there are insufficient

valuation drivers and/or investment catalysts to derive a positive or negative investment view, they may elect with the approval
of Citi Research management not to assign a target price and, thus, not derive an ETR. Citi Research may suspend its rating
and target price and assign “Rating Suspended” status for regulatory and/or internal policy reasons. Citi Research may also
suspend its rating and target price and assign "Under Review" status for other exceptional circumstances (e.g. lack of

information critical to the analyst's thesis, trading suspension) affecting the company and/or trading in the company's
securities. In both such situations, the rating and target price will show as “—“ and “-“ respectively in the rating history price
chart. Prior to 11 April 2022 Citi Research assigned “Under Review” status to both situations and prior to 11 Nov 2020 only in
exceptional circumstances. As soon as practically possible, the analyst will publish a note re-establishing a rating and

investment thesis. Investment ratings are determined by the ranges described above at the time of initiation of coverage, a
change in investment and/or risk rating, or a change in target price (subject to limited management discretion). At times, the
expected total returns may fall outside of these ranges because of market price movements and/or other short-term volatility
or trading patterns. Such interim deviations will be permitted but will become subject to review by Research Management.

Your decision to buy or sell a security should be based upon your personal investment objectives and should be made only
after evaluating the stock's expected performance and risk.
Catalyst Watch/Short Term Views (“STV”) Ratings Disclosure:
Catalyst Watch and STV Upside/Downside calls: Citi Research may also include a Catalyst Watch or STV Upside or Downside
call to indicate the analyst expects the share price to rise (fall) in absolute terms over a specified period of 30 or 90 days in

reaction to one or more specific near-term catalysts or events impacting the company or the market. A Catalyst Watch will be
published when Analyst confidence is high that an impact to share price will occur; it will be a STV when confidence level is
moderate. A Catalyst Watch or STV Upside/Downside call will automatically expire at the end of the specified 30/90 day
period. The Catalyst Watch will also be automatically removed if share price performance (calculated at market close) exceeds

our ratings distribution table for our Catalyst Watch/STV Upside/Downside rating system. However, we reiterate that we do
not consider No View to be a recommendation. For all Catalyst Watch/STV Upside/Downside calls, risk exists that the
catalyst(s) and associated share-price movement will not materialize as expected.

### Page 1

3Q recap - strong op leverage offset GPM pressure — 3Q26 net profits (+125% y/y) 
topped our/BBG estimates 35%/28% respectively owing mostly to stronger sales 
and larger degree of op leverage. Sales reached a record NT$32.4bn (+24% q/q and 
+83% y/y), 23%/12% above Citi/BBG estimate, which can be attributable to strong 
demand in edge AI, semi equipment and smart healthcare applications. GPM fell to 
36.7% (-1ppt q/q and -2.2ppt y/y) below Citi/BBG estimates of 38.0%/37.6%, 
reflecting higher component prices. Nevertheless, OPM expanded to 20.9%, 
underpinned by strong op leverage on higher sales scale. OP profits +151% y/y beat 
Citi/BBG forecast 33%/21%, the key driver to the earnings beat.    
Implications — Strong order intake is encouraging with 3Q BB ratio reaching 1.6x 
and NA particularly robust at 2.1x. However, we believe the stock's rich valuation 
limits further re-rating potential, particularly as a higher comparison base could 
moderate growth in 2027E. Continued GPM compression also reinforces our 
concerns over material/component inflation and the timing of price increases to 
offset higher costs. Advantech will host 3Q26 results call on Nov 4th. We will revisit 
our numbers and share key takeaways following management's updates.

Advantech
Valuation
Our target price of NT$670 is based on 35x 2H26-1H27E EPS, at the high-cycle of the stock's valuation range between 20-35x
since 2020, reflecting solid BB ratio, improving margin profile and ROE.
Risks
Key upside risks that could cause Advantech’s shares to trade above our target price include: 1) better-than-expected
marcoeconomic outlook; and 2) better-than-expected demand and margin profile. Key downside risks would include: 1)

slower-than-expected demand recovery, especially the China market; and 2) further deterioration of demand in US and EU
markets.

Guide to Citi Research Fundamental Research Investment Ratings:
Citi Research stock recommendations include an investment rating and an optional risk rating to highlight high risk stocks.

Risk rating takes into account both price volatility and fundamental criteria. Stocks will either have no risk rating or a High risk
rating assigned.
Investment Ratings: Citi Research investment ratings are Buy, Neutral and Sell. Our ratings are a function of analyst
expectations of expected total return ("ETR") and risk. ETR is the sum of the forecast price appreciation (or depreciation) plus
the dividend yield for a stock within the next 12 months.  The target price is based on a 12 month time horizon. The Investment

rating definitions are: Buy (1) ETR of 15% or more or 25% or more for High risk stocks; and Sell (3) for negative ETR. Any covered
stock not assigned a Buy or a Sell is a Neutral (2). For stocks rated Neutral (2), if an analyst believes that there are insufficient
valuation drivers and/or investment catalysts to derive a positive or negative investment view, they may elect with the approval
of Citi Research management not to assign a target price and, thus, not derive an ETR. Citi Research may suspend its rating

and target price and assign “Rating Suspended” status for regulatory and/or internal policy reasons. Citi Research may also
suspend its rating and target price and assign "Under Review" status for other exceptional circumstances (e.g. lack of
information critical to the analyst's thesis, trading suspension) affecting the company and/or trading in the company's
securities. In both such situations, the rating and target price will show as “—“ and “-“ respectively in the rating history price

chart. Prior to 11 April 2022 Citi Research assigned “Under Review” status to both situations and prior to 11 Nov 2020 only in
exceptional circumstances. As soon as practically possible, the analyst will publish a note re-establishing a rating and
investment thesis. Investment ratings are determined by the ranges described above at the time of initiation of coverage, a
change in investment and/or risk rating, or a change in target price (subject to limited management discretion). At times, the

expected total returns may fall outside of these ranges because of market price movements and/or other short-term volatility
or trading patterns. Such interim deviations will be permitted but will become subject to review by Research Management.
Your decision to buy or sell a security should be based upon your personal investment objectives and should be made only
after evaluating the stock's expected performance and risk.

different from and does not affect a stock’s fundamental equity rating, which reflects a longer-term total absolute return

股價相對大盤走勢

長榮航太 (2645 TT)  Trading Buy

預期波音 737 系列交機速度將略為放緩。  
投資建議 ：雖然 2026 年長榮航太的維修業務持續擴張，但考量
波音將維持 737 系列月均交機一段時間，可能會影響長榮航太
的製造部門復甦情形，故給予長榮航太 Trading Buy 的投資建
議。  
波音 737 系列月交機將維持一段時間，調整長榮航太後續製造
的營運預估： 波音於 10/27/2026 公告 3Q26 財報，並於法說會
上提及 :「當前需維持 737 系列月均交機量穩定在 47 架一段時
間，後續管理階層預期交機提升可能要到 2H27 才會上升至 52
架」，使得長榮航太的製造部門復甦仍需等待一段時間，故研
究部一併調整長榮航太後續的營運預估。  
3Q26 先前的維修漲價陸續貢獻，營運維持高檔： 3Q26 長榮航
太自 2H24 起陸續針對維修業務進行漲價後，加上全球機隊持
續供不應求，增加維修機率與維修產值，惟製造部門雖然波音
已將 737 系列交機上升至 47 架 /月，但短期拉貨力道仍有限，
使得長榮航太的製造業務與轉投資公司營運維持低檔，營運仍
以維修業務為主， 3Q26 營收 52.75 億元， QoQ+1.02% ，預估稅
後純益 8.31 億元， QoQ+10.34% ， EPS 2.22 元。  
2027 年空中巴士首件交付、無人機新訂單，有望成為中長期營
運新動能： 2027 年長榮航太除了持續受惠維修業務與波音交機
的逐年擴張之外，在新訂單的部分，先前與空中巴士的新訂單，
預估 1Q27 將迎來首件交付，並待後續取得特殊製程認證後，
2H27 有望迎來新訂單的量產，以及無人機訂單也有望成為長榮
航太新一波的營運動能，預估 2027 年營收 224.86 億元，
YoY+7.85% ，稅後純益 37.61 億元， YoY+17.06% ，EPS 10.04 元。

3 群益投顧 
圖一、全球商用飛機平均機齡

資料來源 : IATA ，長榮航太  
波音 737 系列月交機將維持一段時間，調整長榮航太後續製造的營運預估：
波音於 10/27/2026 公告 3Q26 財報，並於法說會上提及 :「當前需維持 737 系
列月均交機量穩定在 47 架一段時間，後續管理階層預期交機提升可能要到
2H27 才會上升至 52 架」，使得長榮航太的製造部門復甦仍需等待一段時
間，故研究部一併調整長榮航太後續的營運預估。  
2027 年空中巴士首件交付、無人機新訂單，有望成為中長期營運新動能：  
2027 年長榮航太除了持續受惠維修業務與波音交機的逐年擴張之外，在新
訂單的部分，先前與空中巴士的新訂單，預估 1Q27 將迎來首件交付，並待
後續取得特殊製程認證後， 2H27 有望迎來新訂單的量產，以及無人機訂單
也有望成為長榮航太新一波的營運動能，預估 2027 年營收 224.86 億元，
YoY+7.85% ，稅後純益 37.61 億元， YoY+17.06% ， EPS 10.04 元。  
投資建議：  
雖然 2026 年長榮航太的維修業務持續擴張，但考量波音將維持 737 系列月
均交機一段時間，可能會影響長榮航太的製造部門復甦情形，故給予長榮航
太 Trading Buy 的投資建議。

7 群益投顧 
  【投資評等說明】  
  評等  定義  
  強力買進 (Strong Buy)  首次評等潛在上漲空間 ≥ 35%  
  買進 (Buy)  15% ≤首次評等潛在上漲空間 <35%  
  區間操作 (Trading Buy) 5% ≤首次評等潛在上漲空間 <15%

中立 (Neutral)  
無法由基本面給予投資評等  
預期近期股價將處於盤整  
建議降低持股

【投資評等調整機制】  
  投資評等係「首次」給予特定個股投資評等時，其前一日收盤價相較 3 個月
目標價之潛在上漲空間計算而得。個股投資評等分為四個等級，定義如上。
爾後的投資評等係依循「首次評等」，直到停止推薦。   
「強力買進」、「買進」及「區間操作」均有 upside 目標價。差別僅在於，
首次評等時潛在報酬率不同。「中立」則無目標價。  
一旦我們給予特定個股「強力買進」 、 「買進」或「區間操作」之投資評等，
就是責任的開始，爾後將透過各式研究報告作定期性、持續性追蹤基本面及
股價變化，直到停止推薦。  
停止推薦情境：  
1.  達目標價。  
2.  雖未達原訂目標價，但檢視基本面、訊息面、籌碼面等多方訊息，研
判股價上漲空間已然有限，將適時出具降評報告。  
3.  推薦後股價不漲反跌。

【免責聲明】  
  本研究報告僅提供予特定人之客戶作為參考資料「非經同意不得轉載」 。我
們並不確保此資訊的完整性與正確性，投資人應了解，報告中有關未來預測
之陳述可能不會實現，因而不應被依賴。而且此報告並非根據特定投資目的
或依預定對象之財務狀況所撰寫出來的，因此，此研究報告的目的，既非對
投資人於買賣證券、選擇權、期貨或其他證券相關之衍生性商品提供詢價服
務，亦非作為進行交易的要約。投資人應注意到相關證券之價值及收益，可
能會有無預警地上升或下降，產生投資回報金額可能比原始投資來得少的
情形。

營收走勢圖

研究員/游周翰 Jeffery.Yu@uni-psg.com

研究部重申華星光買進建議，主要考量：(1)管理層維持下半年營運優
於上半年展望，受惠 800G DCI 及 CW Laser 需求持續成長，4Q26 單月
營收有望挑戰 6 億元；(2)隨晶粒稼動率及良率持續改善，研究部上修
2027 年晶粒出貨量預估，加上 EML 等產品逐步放量，有望帶動產品組
合優化及獲利能力提升。綜上所述，研究部預估華星光 2026/2027 年
營收，由原先 60.6 億元/119.8 億元調整為 58.4 億元/120.8 億元
(+33%/107%YoY)，EPS 由原先 7.69 元/15.88 元調整為 7.5 元/16.35
元。考量2027 年產能擴充及相關產品出貨成長，營收與獲利有望呈現
倍數成長，維持目標價 800 元的買進建議。

重點摘要： 
1. 3Q26營收優於研究部預期，惟受匯率影響EPS僅符合預期： 
華星光於 10/14 公 佈 自 結 ，9 月營收 5.75 億元（ +5.3%MoM 、
+97.8%YoY） ，稅後淨利1.03億元，稅後淨利率達17.9%，EPS達
0.72元。相較於8月回升，除了匯率回穩外，亦受到營收規模放
大所影響 。 累計3Q26營收為16.29億元 （+26.4%QoQ，+51.4%YoY），
稅後淨利達3.26億元，EPS為2.28元，創歷史新高。若依研究部
過去費用率及業外假設回推，3Q26毛利率約28-29%（約+6ppts 
QoQ） ，主要受惠先前財稅認列時間差回補、產能利用率提升及
產品組合優化 。 整體而言 ，3Q26營收優於研究部預期16.19億元 ，
EPS僅符合預期2.26元 ， 推測主因受到匯率所影響毛利率及業外
損益。

表一、3Q26 研究部預估與實際差異 
單位：百萬元 實際 研究部原估 差異

### Page 2

請參見末頁重要批露及免責聲明  2

2. 下半年營運優於上半年，惟毛利率受新產品良率等因素影響 ： 
展望4Q26， 管理層維持2H26營運優於1H26， 除了既有DCI產品持
續成長外，CW Laser需求持續提升、800G DCI進入批量生產，
以及EML與高增益APD開始貢獻營收，有望帶動整體營運向上，
單月營收有望挑戰6億元 。 近期經研究部通路訪查 ，800GZR出貨
量略低於原先預估，因此研究部下修 4Q26出貨量從75K下修至
55K，4Q26營收從原先預估19.6億元下修至17.31億元。此外考
量800GZR毛利率因良率及協助客戶購買稀釋毛利率不如原先研
究部預期的情況下，將EPS從2.51元下修至2.29元。

3. 隨產能擴充，營收獲利將持續成長： 
展望未來，在2027年產能倍增情況下，營收規模有望呈現倍數
成長。晶粒方面，除了既有的CW Laser產能擴充外，亦增加EML
晶粒產能，使公司在InP產品線有多種產品分散風險。此外，近
期經供應鏈訪查，晶粒稼動率及良率提升下，有望使整體晶粒
出貨量優於原先預期。因此研究部預估2027年整體晶粒出貨量
從4700萬顆上修至5000萬顆。綜上所述，研究部預估營收2027
年營收從119.8億元上修至120.8億元 ， EPS從15.88元微幅上修
至16.35元。

資料來源： 華星光自結、統一研究部預估

附錄 一 ： 評 等 之 標 準 
評等  定義  
強力買進     預估未來 6 個月內的絕對報酬超過 25%以上  
買進  預估未來 6 個月內的絕對報酬 10% 以上  
中立  預估未來 6 個月內的絕對報酬介於 10～-10%  
降低持股  預估未來 6 個月內的絕對報酬介於 (-10%)以下  
未評等  沒有足夠的基本資料判斷該公司評等

Source PDF: all report pdf/[207940] 삼성바이오로직스(207940) 외형 성장 지속, 수익성 개선은 시간이 필요삼성바이오로직스(207940) 외형 성장 지속, 수익성 개선은 시_dcb09b7c.pdf

### Page 1

www.imeritz.com

삼성바이오로직스 (207940)

Company Brief 
제약/바이오 
Analyst 김준영 
junyoung.kim@meritz.co.kr 
RA 김현비 
hyunbee.kim@meritz.co.kr 외형 성장 지속, 수익성 개선은 시간이 필요

3Q26 Review  
3Q26 연결 기준 매출액 1조 4,903억원(YoY +18.5%, QoQ +12.8%), 영업이익 
6,401억원(YoY +1.0%, QoQ +9.2%)으로 컨센서스를 각각 9.8%, 4.7% 상회했다. 
기존 1 ~ 4공장 풀가동 및 미국 Rockville 공장 매출 약 1,000억원과 5공장 PPQ 
물량 인식이 더해지며 외형 성장을 견인했다. 반면 신규 생산시설의 초기 비용 및 제
품 믹스 영향으로 OPM은 42.9%를 기록하며 전년 동기 대비 7.5%p 하락했다. 동
사는 연간 매출 성장률 가이던스를 기존 +15~20%에서 +20%로 상향했다. 당사는 
4분기 Rockville 공장 매출 기여와 기존 공장의 높은 가동률을 감안하여 연간 매출
액 5조 6,135억원(YoY +23.2%)을 예상한다.

신규 생산거점의 외형 확대, 이익 기여는 좀 더 긴 호흡으로 
5공장은 2Q26부터 PPQ 생산을 본격화했으나 상업 생산에 따른 실질적인 이익 기
여는 2027년 하반기에 확대될 전망이다. Rockville 공장은 3Q와 4Q 각각 약 
1,000억원의 매출이 예상되며 2027년 분기별 매출도 유사한 수준을 유지할 것으로 
전망한다. 다만 영업이익 기여까지는 시간이 더 소요될 것으로 예상되어 2027년 이
익 성장은 미국 공장보다 5공장 Ramp-up이 주도할 전망이다.

투자의견 Buy, 적정주가 200만원 유지 
삼성바이오로직스에 대해 투자의견 Buy, 적정주가 200만원을 유지한다. 고객사 수주 
문의가 회복되는 가운데 대형 계약 협의가 진행 중이며 6공장 역시 연말 또는 내년 
초 착공을 목표로 하고 있다. 다만 신규 수주가 실제 계약으로 이어지는 시점과 5공
장의 상업 생산 전환 속도가 중기 실적의 핵심 변수로 판단한다. 추가 대형 수주 확
보 시 5공장 이후의 성장 가시성도 높아질 것으로 예상한다. 
PolyPeptide 인수는 펩타이드 CDMO로 사업을 확장한다는 점에서 긍정적이다. 인
수 완료 이후 다음 분기부터 연결 매출 인식이 가능하나 유의미한 영업이익 기여까지
는 최소 2년 이상 소요될 전망이다. PolyPeptide 인수는 현재 추정치에서 반영하지 
않았으며 향후 인수 완료 시 연결 OPM 희석 가능성을 감안할 필요가 있다. 한편 최
근 법원 항고심에서 배양부터 정제까지의 생산공정이 쟁의행위 기간에도 정상 수행되
어야 할 보전작업으로 인정되면서 파업에 따른 생산 차질 우려는 완화될 것으로 판단
한다. 노사 협상은 여전히 진행 중이나 생산 안정성에 대한 고객사 우려가 낮아질 경
우 신규 수주에도 긍정적으로 작용할 전망이다. 
 Meritz Research  2026. 10. 22

Buy (20거래일 평균종가 대비 상승 여력 기준)

Report source: Hankyung

### Page 1

HD 현대중공업 (329180/KS) 
우려 감안해도 충분히 매력적 
2026-10-14

조선/방산. 한승한, R.A 고서영 / shane.han@sks.co.kr / 3773-9992

매수(유지) 
목표주가: 820,000 원(하향) 
현재주가: 366,500 원 
상승여력: 123.7%

Signal: 3Q26, 조업일수 및 환율 영향으로 시장 예상치 하회 예상 
Key: 4Q26부터 본격적인 발전엔진 및 FDC 계약 수주 모멘텀 기대 
Step: 상선 우려 감안해도 4 행정 엔진&FDC으로 주가 상승여력 
충분. 매수

3Q26 Preview: 컨센서스 하회하는 실적 기록 예상 
3Q26 연결 매출액 6조 464억원(+36.9% YoY, -4.5% QoQ), 영업이익 9,230억
원(+65.6% YoY, -11.2% QoQ)으로, 시장 예상치(1조 70억원)를 하회하는 실적 기
록할 것으로 추정한다. 이전 분기 대비 중대재해 및 하계휴가, 그리고 추석 연휴에 따른 
조업일수 감소 효과와 더불어 큰 폭의 환율 하락 영향(약 500억원) 때문이다. 홍범도
함 화재 및 임단협 관련 일회성 비용은 이번 분기가 아닌 4Q 에 반영될 것으로 예상한
다. 해양 부문에서 트리온 FPU 프로젝트 C/O에 따른 일부 환입 발생한 것으로 추정
되며, 해양 도크에서 컨테이너선 선체 블록 일부 건조로 고정비 감소효과 일부 상쇄 중
이다. 다만 올해 연내 혹은 내년 초 추가 해양 수주 없을 시 고정비 상승 우려 확대될 
전망이다. 동사 올해 상선 수주 목표(114.7억불)를 이미 초과하는 수주 실적 기록한 상
황이며, 고마진 선종인 LNGC 및 VLGC/VLAC 중심으로 수주했기 때문에 ‘28~’29
년 구간 경쟁사 대비 뚜렷한 선종 믹스 효과를 기대한다.

투자의견 ‘매수’ 유지, 목표주가 82 만원으로 하향 
HD 현대중공업에 대한 투자의견 ‘매수’를 유지하고, 목표주가는 기존 97 만원에서 82
만원으로 하향한다. 중국의 공격적인 캐파 증설 지속과 더불어 중국 대형 조선사들의 
글로벌 선주가 발주한 LNGC 물량 및 원가 경쟁력 확대에 따른 상선 부문의 멀티플 
조정이 불가피하다는 판단이다. 하지만 상선 부문의 우려를 감안하더라도 상선 사이클
과 무관한 육상 발전 엔진 및 FDC에 대한 글로벌 하이퍼스케일러들의 수요 확대는 지
속되고 있는 중으로 파악된다. 올해 4 분기부터 4 행정 중속엔진&FDC 부문의 본격적
인 수주를 통해 중장기적 신성장 동력 확보할 전망이며, 해당 모멘텀에 따른 주가 상승
여력 충분히 남아있다는 판단으로, 조선주 최선호주(Top-Pick)를 유지한다.

본 보고서에 언급된 종목의 경우 당사 조사분석담당자는 본인의 담당종목을 보유하고 있지 않습니다.

당사는 자료공표일 현재 해당기업과 관련하여 특별한 이해 관계가 없습니다.

Company Brief 
이차전지/석유화학 
Analyst 노우호 
wooho.rho@meritz.co.kr 
RA 황현식 
hyeonsig.hwang@meritz.co.kr Long Trading 구간  
3Q26P 잠정실적 발표 이후: ESS ↑, BEV ↓  
LG에너지솔루션의 3Q26P 잠정실적 발표 이후 배터리 섹터는 11/3 미국 중간선거
를 전후로 Trading Long 투자의견이 유효하다. 미국 중간선거 이후 특정정당 우세와 
별개로, 미국 Ex-China ESS 공급망 프리미엄은 유효하다. 동사 역시 그간의 ESS 
사업 난제(단위당 수율, 팩 병목)는 점진적 해소될 점이다. 반면 시장의 BEV 반등 기
대감에 동의하지 않는다. EU 프랑스와 독일의 내연기관 퇴출시점 지연 움직임 및 미
국 연비규제 정책 폐지 결정으로 전통 완성차 고객사들향 BEV 배터리 출하 부진은 
여전하겠다. 미국의 BEV 정책 변화시점은 28.11월 대선으로 지연될 전망이다.

ESS 모멘텀: 한국 제3차 ESS 중앙계약시장 사업자 선정 모멘텀 
대다수 시장 참여자들은 2026년 연말까지 LG에너지솔루션의 미국 ESS 60Gwh  규
모의 잔여 수주 달성 가시성에 집중한다. 해당 ESS 사업 수주 가이던스 관건은 시기
보다 방향성이다. 단, 당사가 주목하는 연말 모멘텀은 한국 정부향 제3차 ESS 중앙
계약시장 사업자 선정으로 판단한다. 3차 규모는 1.13Gw로 1&2차 계약 규모(합산 
1.13Gw) 대비 확대되었다. 또한 언론보도상 LG에너지솔루션은 해당 수주를 위한 핵
심 4대 소재 등 전 Value-chain 국산화를 선언한 바 있다. 해당 수주 확정시, 동사
를 비롯한 해당 Value-chain에 긍정적 모멘텀이 예상된다.

투자의견 Buy, 적정주가 51만원 제시   
26년 연중 지정학 Risk, 에너지 대전환 영향 등에 전력 인프라 수요는 꾸준할 전망
이다. 향후 국내 이차전지 산업은 ‘L’ Shaped Cycle 전망, 반면 동사는 스마트카 T사
향 원통형 전지 출하량 증가, ESS 제조업 이상으로 변모하는 과정으로 산업 평균 성
장률 Outperform 가능하다. 투자의견 Buy 유지, 적정주가는 추정치 상향에 51만원
을 제시한다.

Meritz Research  2026. 10. 15

Buy (20거래일 평균종가 대비 상승 여력 기준)

2 of 8 October 19, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
MACRO STRATEGY
The U.S. Capex Boom No One is Talking About—And Its Implications
Joseph Quinlan, Managing Director and Head of Market Strategy
Ariana Chiu, Assistant Vice President and Investment Strategist
We have long been of the belief that the U.S. is on the cusp of an inbound FDI capex boom
that can result in numerous positive spillover effects for the U.S. economy. The latest

figures from the U.S. Department of Commerce bear this out.
Exhibit 1 highlights the growing push among foreign multinationals to be inside the U.S.,
with FDI totaling nearly $240B in the first six months of this year. That is a staggering
103% jump from a year ago and places FDI inflows on track for a record year.
The latest inbound capex already adds to the massive scale of foreign investment in the
U.S. To wit, the accumulated stock of FDI in the U.S. stands at nearly $6T, based on the

latest figures from the Bureau of Economic Analysis (BEA). Manufacturing accounts for
roughly 43% of the total, and, at last count, some 10,000 foreign affiliates were operating
in the U.S.
These firms are largely invisible to the average U.S. investor, but the activities of U.S.
affiliates of foreign multinationals are substantial and competitive- and growth-enhancing.
To this point and based on the latest data from the BEA, foreign affiliates in the U.S.

employed nearly 8.6 million U.S. workers in 2024. That represents 6.1% of total private
sector employment, with manufacturing and retail the two most prominent sectors
of employment. Roughly 24% of all manufacturing jobs in the U.S. are from foreign
subsidiaries. In terms of pay, U.S. workers at these firms make $89,000 annually, 7% higher
than the national average, according to the Global Business Alliance.
Foreign affiliates also contributed some $1.5T to U.S. GDP; comprised some 15.7% of total

U.S. private sector investment; accounted for 12.4% of total U.S. research & development
(R&D) expenditures; paid roughly 20% of all U.S. federal taxes; and made up 22.5% of
U.S. exports and 26.2% of U.S. imports. 1 These are the direct effects of U.S. subsidiaries
of foreign companies operating in the U.S. The indirect effects are even greater in terms
of more income, jobs, growth and tax revenue—all bolstering the long-term competitive
advantage of the U.S. In a nutshell, foreign firms operating in the U.S. are an important

ingredient of U.S. economic growth.
There are multiple factors behind surging inflows: One, due to rising U.S. trade
protectionism, foreign multinationals increasingly want to be “inside” the U.S. economy,
not outside. Companies that once served the U.S. market exclusively through exports
increasingly face higher costs and more uncertainties and hence want to be “inside.”
Two, with a $32T-plus economy with extraordinary consumer purchasing power, foreign

firms prefer to be closer to their customers via investment than just trade (exports). There
is no more dynamic consumer in the world than the American shopper.
Three, America’s massive investment cycle in AI, data centers, semiconductors, power
generation, etc., has created numerous investment opportunities for many foreign firms
that don’t want to miss out on America’s surging capex cycle.
Four, and finally, abundant energy supplies, deep capital markets and a highly developed

technological ecosystem have also induced more FDI inflows.
As more U.S. affiliates of foreign multinationals set up shop in the U.S., think more job
creation across the country, more manufacturing output, more locally sourced purchases,
more U.S. exports, more spending on R&D, and more tax revenue for local communities.
And over the long run, think a more globally competitive U.S. economy.
1 Activities of U.S. Affiliates of Foreign Multinational Enterprises, 2024” and “Activities of U.S. Affiliates of Foreign

Multinational Enterprises, 2023”, BEA July 2026.
Investment Implications
The boom in foreign investment in
the U.S. should continue to support
employment, productivity, corporate
earnings, and broader U.S. economic
competitiveness, in our view. Hence
our U.S. bias.
Exhibit 1: FDI Inflows to the U.S.

### Page 3

3 of 8 October 19, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
And speaking of global competitiveness, Exhibit 2A is very illustrative of the magnet pull of
the U.S. economy to attract FDI versus the rest of the world. Of new greenfield investment
this decade, note the overriding preference for the U.S., accounting for nearly 16% of total
global greenfield investment this decade. America’s share is larger than the next three
countries combined—the United Kingdom, India, and Germany. And note that America’s

share of new investment is more than 5X that of China’s. Most of this new investment
has gone into manufacturing sectors like electrical equipment, computers, appliances and
batteries and has originated from Asia and Europe.
The bulk of foreign investment comes from Europe, followed by Japan and Canada, but as
Exhibit 2B highlights, there is a new dynamic playing out when it comes to America’s ability
to attract capital. There is a new source in town: a handful of developing nations (namely

Taiwan, South Korea, Mexico and Brazil). Mexican manufacturers, Taiwan technology
leaders, South Korea chip leaders, Brazil industrial firms, India pharmaceutical firms—these
companies and more are realizing that in today’s trade environment, the best way to serve
the American market is not necessarily through exports but through investment—investing
locally. FDI flows from developing nations reached $63B in the 12 months through June,
more than double the level a year prior ($24.5B), according to the BEA. Fueled by insatiable

AI demand, flows from South Korea alone totaled $23.4B in the first half of 2026. The
upshot is that an increasingly important source of capital for the U.S. is coming from an
unlikely source: the developing nations.
More fire for the capex boom. The coming boom in FDI inflows is supportive of our thesis
that the U.S. stands on the cusp of a major industrial upgrade. We are in the early innings
of a structural—not cyclical—capex cycle that should lead to the rebuilding of domestic

supply chains, higher value-added manufacturing, and more AI-driven productivity growth.
Foreign firms, in our opinion, will be part of this process.
That is important to recognize because remember the following: when a foreign company
builds a factory or an R&D facility or expands its existing manufacturing capacity right
here in the U.S., good things happen. Demand for U.S. goods and services goes up (as does
demand for construction, materials, logistics and professional services). New jobs are created,

boosting incomes of U.S. workers, while supporting more local spending and consumption.
And because many foreign firms often bring advanced manufacturing technologies when
investing in the U.S., the end results include a boost in productivity and innovation in various
sectors of the economy.
All the above, of course, are the secret ingredients to stronger U.S. economic growth, rising
corporate earnings and higher equity prices. With a little help from our friends, America’s

industrial renewal is gaining strength and momentum.

MARKET VIEW
Europe’s Electoral Cycle and Market Risks
Ehiwario Efeyini, Director and Senior Investment Strategist
As midterm elections approach for the U.S. next month, voters across Europe will also
enter their own election season starting this November and continuing throughout
2027. Over the next year, 52% of the total EU population, representing 46% of EU GDP
across eight countries, will go to the polls, including voters in three of its four largest

economies (Exhibit 3). Having announced snap general elections earlier this month,
Spain will vote for members of its lower and upper house on November 29. A series of
parliamentary elections are then due for 2027 in Estonia (by March 7), Finland (on April
18), Greece (by July 25), Slovakia (by September 28), Poland (by November 11) and Italy
(by December 22). And perhaps most consequentially France will stage Presidential
elections on April 18, with a potential second round of voting set for May 2.

For the EU as a whole, the significance of these coming elections will be the potential
for a shift toward more populist, domestically oriented policy priorities from leading
opposition candidates (particularly in France, Spain and Poland) which could obstruct the
process of closer EU integration. In the nearer term, adoption of the new seven-year
(2028-2034) EU budget due by the end of 2027 could be impeded. And over the longer
term, renewed efforts toward an EU-wide capital market union to deepen and attract

more inward investment into European markets could also be further delayed.
At the country level, investors have seen bond yields rise globally in 2026 on the back of
higher inflation and concerns over fiscal sustainability. The debt question has arguably
been most pressing in parts of Europe, especially given the likelihood that upcoming
elections make for domestic increases in government largesse. And of the eight
countries going to the polls over the next year, France remains the biggest source of

concern. Interest rate spreads on French 10-year sovereign bonds have overtaken those
in both Greece and Italy this year (Exhibit 4), driven by investor concerns over public
debt. The 2027 government budget unveiled earlier this month projected a deficit of
5.0% of GDP (well in excess of the 3% specified in the EU Stability and Growth Pact),
but remained insufficiently expansive to nullify the risk that the current government
could be unseated later this year if it is forced through the fragmented parliament. This

comes on top of an existing debt load of close to 120% of GDP and a halving of official
2026 growth forecasts from 1.0% at the start of the year to 0.5% in September. In
short, all four variables in France’s debt sustainability equation currently stand as major
headwinds, and the two leading candidates in the 2027 election have shown limited
appetite for fiscal consolidation over the coming years.

Investment Implications
We remain cautious on European
markets overall, despite seeing
pockets of strength in selected
areas such as defense, data center
infrastructure and renewable energy.
On top of growing uncertainty
from the upcoming electoral cycle

and interest rate outlook, rising
competition and slowing demand
from China continue to challenge
key segments of the Consumer
Discretionary sector. And limited
direct AI concentration also leaves
Europe under-exposed to the key
growth theme that continues to buoy
equity markets in the U.S., Japan and
emerging Asia.

Intervention from the ECB under its 2022 bond purchase program is also unlikely at this

stage given that the spread widening in France has not appeared either disorderly or
unwarranted. Meanwhile, conditions could potentially be aggravated by expected ECB rate
hikes in response to still above-target inflation across the eurozone. Indeed recent moves
to tighten monetary policy from a number of major central banks have been read most
hawkishly in Europe. Most of the increase in European bond yields has come from higher
inflation breakevens as opposed to real yields in the U.S. and Japan. And despite some

paring back of tightening expectations in the wake of recent bond volatility, the market is
still pricing three more policy rate increases over the next 12 months.
Euro depreciation and an emerging shift toward more protectionist trade policy with China
could further complicate the ECB policy outlook. China posted a record $35B trade surplus,
according to Eurostat, with the EU in September (alongside its $938B total surplus for the
first three quarters of 2026), and the two markets have agreed to reduce the EU bilateral

deficit by capping imports of hybrid cars for four years. Hybrids account for less than 2%
of total EU imports from China, but this could potentially mark the start of a broader shift
toward more trade protection in Europe with its largest foreign supplier which risks adding
further upward pressure to cost-push inflation. And though the leading candidates in the
French election may favor resuming oil and gas imports from Russia, Europe also remains

among the most at risk from the global energy shock. Indeed some 56% of the region’s
crude oil consumption is in the form of diesel product according to the International Energy
Agency, more than twice the U.S. share.
The 2- to 10-year eurozone yield curve has already flattened significantly since the start
of the year. And at just over 30 basis points as of mid-October, it remains flatter than
its equivalents in the U.S., UK and Japan. Outside Energy and Technology, Financials have

been among the strongest-performing sectors in Europe this year. But additional ECB
rate hikes could lead to further curve flattening, compressing bank net interest margins
and contributing to slower growth—particularly given that rate increases are coming in
response to supply-driven inflation rather than reflecting stronger economic activity.
We nonetheless continue to see pockets of strength in European markets. Growing
defense budgets should remain a tailwind for Industrials as national governments look to

meet their North Atlantic Treaty Organization targets over the next several years. And the
sector should be further supported by indirect exposure to AI investment through electric
cabling, power infrastructure and building materials for data centers. Leaders in renewable
energy equipment and services are also likely to be beneficiaries of grid upgrades and
broadening electrification globally, particularly given elevated fossil fuel prices. But our view

on the region remains cautious overall. On top of growing uncertainty from the upcoming
electoral cycle and interest rate outlook, rising competition and slowing demand from
China continue to challenge key segments of Europe’s Consumer Discretionary sector.
Limited direct AI concentration in technology hardware, cloud services and application
software also leaves Europe underexposed to the key growth theme that continues to buoy
equity markets in the U.S., Japan and emerging Asia.

### Page 6

6 of 8 October 19, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
THOUGHT OF THE WEEK
Cautious Optimism Entering Fiscal Year 2027
Ariana Chiu, Assistant Vice President and Investment Strategist
“A renewed focus on rebuilding fiscal space is urgent, given elevated debt, higher
borrowing costs, multiplying demands on public budgets and heightened uncertainty.” So
warned the IMF about the global fiscal outlook last week in its updated World Economic

Outlook report. Much is at stake for developed nations in Europe and Asia facing high
existing debt, unfavorable demographics and more modest growth.
For the U.S., between structurally higher mandatory outlays, the administration’s $1.5T
proposed defense package, and the 10-year U.S. Treasury yield sitting above 5%, spending
is likely headed higher. We continue to fade concerns of an impending U.S. debt crisis,
however, largely because economic growth continues to surprise to the upside. Expanding

output is good news for the debt-to-GDP ratio, which remains a touch below 100%.
Indeed, rather than being led higher on account of un-anchored inflation expectations, the
10-year U.S. Treasury yield has repriced to reflect better-than-expected nominal growth
(Exhibit 5A). Higher yields in the face of slowing growth, meanwhile, would be a different
story, not only for the fiscal outlook (worse) but also for U.S. equity valuations (lower).
Of course, after the U.S. accumulated another $2T deficit in FY 2026, 2 “rebuilding fiscal

space” in the IMF’s words will be politically unpopular and mathematically challenging.
In our view, the biggest near-term tail risk would be a surprise move lower in growth as
budget deficits at 6% of GDP in good times leave little room for bad times. This isn’t our
base case given robust consumer spending per our internal credit and debit card data and
ongoing investment in the AI buildout and beyond. We expect these two pillars to sustain
growth in 2027.

The other good news is that, contrary to concerns, demand for Treasurys remains robust.
Yields are attractive—that was the message from October’s successful $39B 10-year
Treasury note auction. At the same time, U.S. debt boasts an array of buyers: domestic
households, the Federal Reserve, mutual funds and exchange-traded funds, money market
funds, pension funds, foreign central banks, private investors overseas, and more. Per
Exhibit 5B, whereas foreign official investors once dominated some 40% of the U.S.

Treasury market during the 2008/2009 Great Financial Crisis, their share has fallen to
12% while domestic private investors now account for a majority of holdings. Translated:
demand is more diverse than most appreciate.
Exhibit 5: Higher Yields Meet Shifting Composition of U.S. Treasury Demand.

Investment Implications
In our view, higher nominal and real
yields present a compelling entry point
for investors seeking income and can

rate volatility. We expect demand for
U.S. assets to remain healthy and favor
the U.S. over developed economies in
Europe facing more modest growth and
fiscal instability.

of America, Merrill or any affiliate to sell or provide, or a specific invitation for a consumer to apply for, any particular retail financial product or service that may be available.

Investments have varying degrees of risk. Some of the risks involved with equity securities include the possibility that the value of the stocks may fluctuate in response to events specific to

the companies or markets, as well as economic, political or social events in the U.S. or abroad. Small cap and mid cap companies pose special risks, including possible illiquidity and greater price
volatility than funds consisting of larger, more established companies. Investing in fixed-income securities may involve certain risks, including the credit quality of individual issuers, possible
prepayments, market or economic developments and yields and share price fluctuations due to changes in interest rates. When interest rates go up, bond prices typically drop, and vice versa.

Investments in high-yield bonds (sometimes referred to as “junk bonds”) offer the potential for high current income and attractive total return, but involves certain risks. Changes in economic
conditions or other circumstances may adversely affect a junk bond issuer’s ability to make principal and interest payments. Income from investing in municipal bonds is generally exempt
from Federal and state taxes for residents of the issuing state. While the interest income is tax-exempt, any capital gains distributed are taxable to the investor. Income for some investors

may be subject to the Federal Alternative Minimum Tax (AMT). Treasury bills are less volatile than longer-term fixed income securities and are guaranteed as to timely payment of principal and
interest by the U.S. government. Bonds are subject to interest rate, inflation and credit risks. Investments in foreign securities (including ADRs) involve special risks, including foreign currency
risk and the possibility of substantial volatility due to adverse political, economic or other developments. These risks are magnified for investments made in emerging markets. Investments in

a certain industry or sector may pose additional risk due to lack of diversification and sector concentration. There are special risks associated with an investment in commodities, such as gold,
including market price fluctuations, regulatory changes, interest rate changes, credit risk, economic changes and the impact of adverse political or financial factors.
Alternative investments are speculative and involve a high degree of risk.
Alternative investments are intended for qualified investors only. Alternative Investments such as derivatives, hedge funds, private credit, private equity funds, and funds of funds can result

in higher return potential but also higher loss potential. Changes in economic conditions or other circumstances may adversely affect your investments. Before you invest in alternative
investments, you should consider your overall financial situation, how much money you have to invest, your need for liquidity, and your tolerance for risk.

Weekly Economics
U.S. Week Ahead
Existing Home Sales • Thursday
Housing market conditions remain highly unfavorable. Mortgage
rates are the primary headwind, and they have climbed even higher
alongside the rise in longer-term yields. At the same time, low
supply is preventing a broad correction in home prices, leaving
prospective buyers squeezed on two fronts.
We expect that existing home sales slid 3.4% in August to an
annualized rate of 3.92 million. Behind this forecast is ongoing

weakness in mortgage applications and a recent bounce in
mortgage rates, which rose from 6.43% at the beginning of July to
6.66% by month-end.
We do not expect affordability conditions to meaningfully improve
in the near term. Supply is likely to remain constrained while high
mortgage rates discourage mobility. And mortgage rates are apt
to remain elevated amid firm economic growth expectations and
heavy government and corporate debt issuance.

### Page 3

Economics Week Ahead Economics
CPI • Friday
Headline inflation looks to have picked up last month. We estimate
the CPI rose 0.40% in August, with gasoline prices increasing a little
over 4% as renewed tensions in the Middle East pushed oil higher.
Grocery prices also likely rebounded after modestly declining in July.
Outside of food and energy, price pressures appear little changed.
We expect the core CPI to rise 0.23%, essentially matching July's

pace. Core goods inflation should remain firm, led by another solid
increase in used vehicle prices as consumer prices continue to
catch up to the trend indicated at wholesale auctions. Meantime,
household furnishings and other tariff-sensitive categories appear
to be stabilizing after declining earlier this year.
Core services inflation should be little changed with another 0.2%
monthly increase. But underneath the surface, we expect a modest
pickup in travel (led by lodging away from home) to offset slightly

cooler medical care and housing cost growth.
All told, we expect the report to show that while headline inflation
remains influenced by the ebbs and flows of the conflict in the
Middle East, core inflation remains contained. Should our forecast
prove correct, core CPI will ease to 2.4% year-over-year, in line with
its 6-month annualized rate but above the more recent 3-month
annualized pace of 1.7%.

### Page 4

Weekly Economics
G10 Week Ahead
European Central Bank Monetary Policy Meeting •
Thursday
We expect the European Central Bank (ECB) to raise the Deposit
Rate by 25 bps to 2.50% next week. August headline inflation
remained elevated at 3.3% year-over-year, though softer core
and services inflation suggest underlying pressures continue to
moderate. Still, the more hawkish tone of the July minutes and
renewed rise in energy prices should keep policymakers cautious.

The recent escalation in the Middle East has pushed fuel prices
at the pump close to their 2022 peaks, while natural gas storage
remains below historical levels, leaving the Eurozone vulnerable
to further energy price increases. The ECB is unlikely to respond
aggressively if the shock remains confined to energy given the
downside risks to growth. However, a broader rise in food inflation
linked to El Niño would be more concerning, as highly visible prices

could lift inflation expectations and generate second-round effects.
For now, resilient Q3 activity, elevated headline inflation and
contained wage growth support one final hike. The ECB will likely
retain and emphasize its scenario-based outlook in the updated
staff projections given continued uncertainty around the conflict
and energy prices. The policy path beyond September will depend
on whether energy and food pressures persist and begin to spread,

which could leave room for further tightening.

U.K. Monthly GDP • Friday
We expect UK GDP to rise 0.2% month-over-month in July, with
growth of 0.3% on a three-month-over-three-month basis.

Activity likely moderated after June’s strong rebound, though
services remained supportive as the July services PMI rose to 52.1.
Retail sales volumes fell 0.5% month-over-month, while softer
manufacturing activity likely reflected some reversal of earlier
precautionary stockbuilding.
The moderation appears more consistent with payback from
June than a broader deterioration in momentum. August PMIs
remain consistent with Q3 GDP growth of around 0.3% quarter-

over-quarter. Still, the renewed escalation in the Middle East and
associated rise in gas and energy prices adds upside risk to inflation
and could weigh on household demand. Against this backdrop and
with Bank of England hawks continuing to emphasize the need for
tighter policy, we expect the BoE to remain cautious and continue
to look for a rate hike in Q4. -1.0%

Economics Week Ahead Economics
EM Week Ahead
Mexico CPI • Wednesday
We expect Mexico’s August inflation rate to edge higher, with
headline CPI rising to 3.34% year-over-year (0.27% month-over-
month) from 3.26% year-over-year in early August. Part of the
pickup likely reflects less favorable base effects and a smaller
disinflationary contribution from food prices. While agricultural
prices continued to help restrain inflation in mid-August, the drag

was notably less pronounced than in July. Core inflation should
remain elevated at 3.98% year-over-year (0.25% month-over-
month).
While inflation remains within Banxico’s midpoint 3% target,
persistent core and services inflation suggest convergence toward
the midpoint of the target could prove gradual. For now, we
continue to expect Banxico to keep rates on hold, with the policy
rate remaining at 6.50% through the rest of 2026 and into early
2027 as policymakers await clearer evidence that underlying

inflation pressures are easing sustainably. While rate cuts remain
a possibility in 2027, the current inflation backdrop argues for
patience in the near term.

Brazil CPI • Friday
We expect Brazil’s August inflation rate to slow to 4.20% year-over-
year, helped by lower electricity, food and fuel prices. The main
driver should be a temporary reduction in residential electricity bills
stemming from a one-off credit, while recent data already point to
a notable easing in price pressures. Mid-August CPI came in weaker
than expected, with inflation falling to 4.24% year-over-year amid
declines in electricity, food, fuel and airfare prices. Beyond these

temporary factors, restrictive monetary policy continues to weigh
on economic activity, as evidenced by the recent slowdown in Q2
GDP growth. However, underlying inflation risks remain. A still-
tight labor market and government measures aimed at supporting
consumption could keep services inflation sticky, making progress
toward the 3.0% inflation target more gradual.
Overall, the inflation outlook remains consistent with additional
policy easing. We expect COPOM to deliver a 25 bps rate cut in

September, bringing the Selic Rate to 13.75%. Beyond September,
policymakers are likely to proceed cautiously as they assess
incoming inflation data and the impact of the election cycle on
inflation expectations. Our base case remains for a pause after
September, with additional easing more likely in 2027.

Document 295: Entering the age of the older consumer As America ages, older consumers have more leisure time, significant wealth and growing economic influence. 2 min read

Source PDF: all report pdf/Entering the age of the older consumer As America ages, older consumers have more leisure time, significant wealth and growing economic influence. 2 min read.pdf
Report source: Bank of America
Date: 2026-10-01
Pages: 8
Status: ok

### Page 1

13023135     1

Economy 
Entering the age of the older consumer 
01 October 2026

Key takeaways 
• The older consumer is moving center stage. America's population has been aging for decades, but the shift has accelerated: the 
share aged over 60 increased by almost 10 percentage points (pp) between 1995 and 2025. And the Census Bureau projects the 
share of the population over 60 will rise another 5pp by 2055, to stand at nearly 30% of the population. 
• More free time means a different spending mix. People aged 65 and over spend around two and a half fewer hours per day 
working and roughly two hours more on leisure than the overall adult population. Bank of America data shows that older 
households allocate relatively more of their card spending to groceries and travel, and less to restaurants, gasoline, general 
merchandise and clothing. 
• Households aged over 55 held close to $140 trillion in net worth in the second quarter of 2026, giving many older consumers 
considerable capacity to spend on travel and other leisure categories. But wealth is not evenly distributed, and healthcare and 
other cost pressures remain significant for older households on tighter or fixed incomes.

Population aging is nothing new, but it is accelerating  
The US population has been growing relatively “older” for a long time. Exhibit 1 shows that the proportion of the population 
under 20 years old shrank from the mid-1960s through 2025. At the same time, the proportion of the population in older age 
groups has been rising.  
Exhibit 1: Over time the US population has steadily aged  
Proportion of US population by age range (%)

Source: Human Mortality Database; Bank of America Institute calculations

Additionally, there has been a notable acceleration in the relative aging of the population in recent years. For example, between 
1995 and 2025, the proportion of the population aged over 60 rose by almost 10 percentage points (pp) to 25%.  
Several factors have driven this. First, fertility rates in the US, as in much of the world, have fallen sharply. This has slowed 
natural population growth and, by reducing the number of younger people relative to older cohorts, has increased the share of 
the population at older ages. Second, more recently, net inward migration to the US has slowed. Because migrants tend to be 
younger than the overall population, weaker immigration also tends to raise the average age. Finally, rising life expectancy over 
much of the post-war period has allowed more people to survive into older age, further increasing the older population's share.  
Exhibit 2: The trend towards an older composition of the population is expected to continue  
Forecast changes in the share of population by age group relative to 2025 (pp)

Source: United States Census Bureau 
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE 
Lower fertility and, more recently, weaker net migration look set to slow overall US population growth in the decades ahead. As a 
result, older Americans are likely to play an increasingly important role in the economy, both as a share of the population and, in 
many age groups, in absolute numbers. The Census Bureau projects the share of the population over 60 will rise another 5pp by 
2055, to stand at nearly 30% of the population (Exhibit 2). For businesses, understanding how the needs and spending patterns 
of older consumers differ from those of younger generations will therefore become increasingly relevant. 
Older age groups allocate their time differently  
Why does this matter? One reason is that older people tend to spend their time and money quite differently from younger adults. 
Most obviously, many work less or have retired altogether. Data from the American Time Use Survey (ATUS) shows that people 
aged 65 and over spend, on average, around two and a half fewer hours per day working than the population aged 15+ (Exhibit 
3). 
That leaves considerably more time for other activities. People aged 65 and over spend around two hours more per day on 
leisure and sports activities, with television viewing accounting for a particularly large share of this additional leisure time 
(Exhibit 4).

Older households also spend differently  
Bank of America internal data on credit and debit card spending provides insights into how these differences in older 
generations’ use of time also impact their spending. Rather than splitting our spending data by generation, as we often do, here 
we split by age bands, allowing for consistent comparisons over time.  
Exhibit 5 shows how the composition of card spending of households aged 61-75 and those older than 75 differs from that of all 
households. A few things stand out. For one, older households devote a greater share of their overall card spending to groceries 
(food and beverages). Households aged 61-75 also allocate more spending to travel (airlines and lodging). And restaurants and 
bars, gasoline, general merchandise and clothing account for a smaller share of older households’ card spending.  
These differences may seem fairly intuitive. Older households have more leisure hours, so spend relatively more on travel to 
enjoy them. Previously we found that more available time may support travel particularly around retirement, although the 
discretionary spending lift can be temporary (for more, read our publication: Paychecks to pensions: The evolution of retiree 
spending).  
Older households are also likely to have less need to update their work wardrobes, so they probably spend less on clothing.

Still, older households’ relatively lower spending on restaurants and bars is a little more curious, perhaps, given dining out could 
be one way of making use of more leisure time. Indeed, when we look at how these spending shares are evolving, we find a 
nuanced picture. While households aged 61-75 do spend relatively less on restaurants and bars than the overall population, they 
appear to be narrowing this gap. But for those older than 75 this is not the case. In our view, the pandemic may still be weighing 
on dining-out spending among this older age group (Exhibit 6).

Aging presents opportunities, but also challenges 
In our view, the aging of the US population presents important opportunities for the economy and businesses. Older consumers 
have more leisure time and businesses that cater to these demands may therefore benefit. 
Moreover, some older consumers hold considerable amounts of net wealth and have been some of the biggest beneficiaries of 
recent rises in equities. Using data from the Federal Reserve, Exhibit 8 shows that as of the second quarter of 2026 the net 
worth of those households aged 55 and over was close to $140 trillion, around three-quarters of the total. And with equity 
markets rising strongly, these age groups have seen a more than 20% increase in their net worth over the past two years, 
potentially enhancing their spending power further.  
But an important caveat is that not all older consumers are in this position. Previous Institute work found evidence consistent 
with equity-market wealth effects being concentrated toward higher-income households (read our publication: Paychecks to 
pensions: The evolution of retiree spending). And Census Bureau research has found that around 14% of Social Security 
recipients aged over 65 rely on it for over 90% of their income. For older households without a wealth cushion, recent elevated 
cost pressures, like gasoline, will likely have put them under more significant financial strain. They will also be more dependent 
on cost-of-living (COLA) increases in Social Security to underpin their spending.  
And, more broadly, outside of card spending, healthcare costs are a much larger factor in older households’ budgets (Exhibit 9). 
The evolution of these costs going forward will be a key variable for older consumers’ budgets.

Methodology  
Selected Bank of America transaction data is used to inform the macroeconomic views expressed in this report and should be considered in the 
context of other economic indicators and publicly available information. In certain instances, the data may provide directional and/or predictive 
value. The data used is not comprehensive; it is based on aggregated and anonymized selections of Bank of America data and may reflect a 
degree of selection bias and limitations on the data available. 
Any payments data represents aggregated spend from US Retail, Preferred, Small Business and Wealth Management clients with a deposit 
account or credit card. Aggregated spend include total credit card, debit card, ACH, wires, bill pay, business/peer-to-peer, cash, and checks.  
Any Small Business payments data represents aggregate spend from Small Business clients with a deposit account or a Small Business credit 
card. Payroll payments data include channels such as ACH (automated clearing house), bill pay, checks and wire. Bank of America per Small 
Business client data represents activity spending from active Small Business clients with a deposit account or a Small Business credit card and 
at least one transaction in each month. Small businesses in this report include business clients within Bank of America and generally defined as 
under $5mm in annual sales revenue.  
Unless otherwise stated, data is not adjusted for seasonality, processing days or portfolio changes, and may be subject to periodic revisions.  
The differences between the total and per household card spending growth rate (if discussed) can be explained by the following reasons:  
1. Overall total card spending growth is partially boosted by the growth in the number of active cardholders in our sample. This could be due to 
an increasing customer base or inactive customers using their cards more frequently. 
2. Per household card spending growth only looks at households that complete at least five transactions with Bank of America cards in the 
month. Per household spending growth isolates impacts from a changing sample size, which could be unrelated to underlying economic 
momentum, and potential spending volatility from less active users. 
3. Overall total card spending includes small business card spending while per household card spending does not. 
4. Differences due to using processing dates (total card spending) versus transaction date (per household card spending). 
5. Other differences including household formations due to young adults moving in and out of their parent’s houses during COVID. 
Any household consumer deposit data based on Bank of America internal data is derived by anonymizing and aggregating data from Bank of 
America consumer deposit accounts in the US and analyzing that data at a highly aggregated level. Whenever median household savings and 
checking balances are quoted, the data is based on a fixed cohort of households that had a consumer deposit account (checking and/or savings 
account) for all months from January 2019 through the most current month of data shown.  
Bank of America aggregated credit/debit card spending per household includes spending from active US households only. Only consumer card 
holders making a minimum of five transactions a month are included in the dataset. Spending from corporate cards are excluded. Data 
regarding merchants who receive payments are identified and classified by the Merchant Categorization Code (MCC) defined by financial 
services companies. The data are mapped using proprietary methods from the MCCs to the North American Industry Classification System 
(NAICS), which is also used by the Census Bureau, in order to classify spending data by subsector. Spending data may also be classified by other 
proprietary methods not using MCCs.  
We consider a measure of services necessity spending that includes but is not limited to groceries, utilities, and gasoline. Discretionary services 
includes but is not limited to charitable donations, leisure travel, entertainment, and professional/consumer services. Discretionary retail 
includes but is not limited to general merchandise, miscellaneous, clothing, electronics and furniture. It excludes categories like groceries and 
gasoline. Holiday spending is defined as items in which spending in the November-December period is usually at least 20% of total annual 
spending on the category.  Value and premium grocers are determined judgmentally on a proprietary analysis of merchants by equity analysts.  
Durables spending is defined as spending on electronics, building materials, auto-related spending and furniture. Premium durables spending is 
based on a selection of retailers who are judged to sell relatively higher value products. Conversely, value durables spending is based on a 
selection of retailers who are judged to sell relatively lower value products.  
Lower, middle and higher household income cuts in Bank of America credit and debit card spending per household, and consumer deposit 
account data are based on quantitative estimates of each households’ income. These quantitative estimates are bucketed according to terciles, 
with a third of households placed in each tercile periodically. The lowest tercile represents ‘lower income’, the middle tercile represents ‘middle 
income’ and the highest tercile ‘higher income’. The income thresholds between these terciles will move over time, reflecting any number of 
factors that impact income, including general wage inflation, changes in social security payments and individual households’ income. The 
income and tercile in which a household is categorised are periodically re-assessed.  
Generations, if discussed, are defined as follows: Gen Z, born after 1995; Younger Millennials: born between 1989-1995; Older Millennials: born 
between 1978-1988; Gen Xers: born between 1965-1977; Baby Boomer: 1946-1964; Traditionalists: pre-1946. 
Any reference to card spending per household on gasoline includes all purchases at gasoline stations and might include purchases of non-gas 
items. 
Additional information about the methodology used to aggregate the data is available upon request.

Document 309: France_ double-digit growth in business creations, a slowdown in the rise in bankruptcies and stable non-performing loans ratio (2026-10-08)

Source PDF: all report pdf/France_ double-digit growth in business creations, a slowdown in the rise in bankruptcies and stable non-performing loans ratio (2026-10-08).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-10-08
Pages: 1
Status: ok

### Page 1

Paris, 8 October 2026

EcoFlash

France: double-digit growth in business creations, a slowdown in the rise in bankruptcies and 
stable non-performing loans ratio

Business creations rose by 12% y/y over the first eight months of 2026 1. This is significantly higher than the 5.3% y/y 
growth recorded in 2025. This increase was driven by sectors linked to information and communication technologies.

Meanwhile, business bankruptcies rose by 5.3% y/y between January and July, according to data from the Banque de 
France (compared with the 3.6% y/y increase for the whole of 2025). The rise was more pronounced in the 1st quarter (+8% y/y) 
before slowing down. This slowdown could persist, as, according to Altares, payment delays have fallen to an average of 12.3 
days in the second quarter of 2026, compared with 14.1 days a year earlier (and 13.1 days for the European average).

Rising bankruptcies are more heavily affecting the services sector, which is also experiencing slightly stronger growth 
in business creations. This correlation is particularly noticeable in the retail sector, as business creations have risen by 13% 
since the start of 2026, while bankruptcies are up by 3%. In construction and manufacturing, business bankruptcies have been 
falling since the second quarter of 2026.

The average ratio of non -performing corporate loans has remained stable at a moderately high level for the past year  
(4.0% of total loans and advances in Q2 2026). In construction, where activity is more cyclical than in other sectors, the ra tio of 
non-performing loans has reached its highest level since 2019, standing at 9.5%.

The proportion of loans that have deteriorated but are not yet non-performing (“Stage 2” according to IFRS 9 terminology) 
is stable, standing at 15.4% q/q, and remains below its all -time high (16.1%). This indicator, which helps to anticipate trends in 
non-performing loans, is relatively encouraging.

Despite a significant increase since 2021, the economic weight of bankruptcies remains, on average, below its peak in 
Q1 2025 (0.53 compared with 0.61, respectively). This indicator generally allows bankruptcies to be weighted based on the size 
of the businesses. However, the economic weight of bankrupted small businesses is at a historically high level (2.11).

Take a look at our EcoCharts on trends in business creations and bankruptcies in France and the 
associated risks. It will be updated on a quarterly basis.

Thomas Humblot 
Economiste, team: Banking economics 
thomas.humblot@bnpparibas.com

Stéphane Colliac 
Head of Advanced economies, France 
stephane.colliac@bnpparibas.com

1 For more details, see Business creations and bankruptcies in France: double-digit growth in business creations, lower growth in bankruptcies, 
and stable non-performing loans, 8 October 2026.

### Page 2

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
2
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
BULLETIN
The six-month annualised growth rate in the Westpac–
Melbourne Institute Leading Index, which indicates the
likely pace of economic activity relative to trend three to
nine months into the future, held at –0.03% in September,
unchanged from August.

The Leading Index continues to show moderately improved
momentum compared to the middle of the year, with the
latest reads pointing to growth holding at or slightly below
trend heading into late 2026/early 2027. Growth appears
to be stabilising at a slow pace rather than stalling. That
said, both the latest fuel prices and the RBA’s September
interest rate increase are still working their way through to
the economy with impacts likely to take some of the gloss off

growth near term. Overall, Westpac expects annual growth
to come in at 1.5% for calendar 2026, a below-trend result.
The Leading Index growth rate has firmed slightly over the
last six months, rising from –0.16% back in March to –0.03%
in September. The component detail shows mixed moves over
the period. Four components have contributed more positively
with reduced drags from the Westpac-Melbourne Institute
Unemployment Expectations Index (adding +0.23ppts to

the Index growth rate); the Westpac-Melbourne Institute
Consumer Expectations Index (adding another +0.15ppts);
and the S&P/ASX200 (+0.15ppts) combining with a modest
lift in growth in US industrial production (adding +0.12ppts).
These positives have been partially offset by modest drags
from four previously stable components: dwelling approvals
(–0.34ppts); monthly hours worked (–0.08ppts); commodity
prices, measured in AUD terms (–0.07ppts) and the yield

spread (–0.05ppts).
Overall, the component mix speaks to a lack of direction.
Indeed, despite some large shocks over the last year, the
Leading Index growth rate has been relatively contained since
early 2025, holding in a narrow –0.3% to +0.5% range. This
compares to history which, outside of the COVID and GFC
shocks, has seen growth rates move between a much wider
–1.9% to +1.6% range. Of course, the apparent stability,
which is evident across all components of the index, may not

last but for now it suggests the economy has been stuck in a
bit of a holding pattern over the last two years.
Leading Index still tracking a touch below trend
The Westpac-Melbourne Institute Leading Index of
Economic Activity is designed to assist in identifying
turning points in the economy. The index combines
variables that reflect different aspects of the economy
into a single index that generally produces a more reliable
cyclical indicator than any single component taken

individually. The Leading Index of Economic Activity
provides advance information on the state of the economy
and gives early warnings of cyclical turning points.
Matthew Hassan
Head of Australian Macro-Forecasting
